\displaystyle\llbracket A\wedge\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cap\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cap\emptyset^{X}

\displaystyle=\emptyset^{X}.

•

Let
\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q
.
Since
\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X})\big)<q\}
= (
2^{\omega}
)
X
,

\displaystyle\llbracket A\wedge\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cap\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cap(2^{\omega})^{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}.

Therefore

\llbracket A\wedge\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}=\begin{cases}\emptyset^{X}\ \ &\text{ if }\mu(\Pi(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))>q\\
\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\ \ &\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(\neg A\vee B)\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\vee B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

\displaystyle=\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}\cup\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

\displaystyle=\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cup\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

There are two cases:

•

Let
\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\geq q
. Since
\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cup\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)

\geq

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)
,

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cup\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\geq q
.
Consequently,
\{f\in
(
2^{\omega}
)
X

|

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cup\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}
=
\emptyset^{X}
.

•

Let
\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q
.

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(\neg A\vee B)\rrbracket_{X}=\

\displaystyle\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \cup\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))<q\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{C}\penalty\ \ref{C1}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ f\not\in\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \wedge\mu(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))<q\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ f\not\in\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X}\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(\neg A\vee B)\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\emptyset^{X}\ \ &\text{ if }\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\geq q\\
\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X}\ \ &\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\wedge\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}.

Let finally prove,

\llbracket A\vee\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}=\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(\neg A\wedge B)\rrbracket_{X}.

For Definition
4.3
,

\llbracket A\vee\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cup\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}.

Let
f\in
(
2^{\omega}
)
X
. There are two possible cases,

•

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\geq q
.
Since
\{f\in
(
2^{\omega}
)
X

|

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\}

=
\emptyset^{X}
,

\displaystyle\llbracket A\vee\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cup\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cup\emptyset^{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}

•

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q
.
Since
\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X})\big)<q\}
=
(2^{\omega})^{X}
,

\displaystyle\llbracket A\vee\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cup\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\cup(2^{\omega})^{X}

\displaystyle=(2^{\omega})^{X}.

Therefore,

\llbracket A\vee\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}=\begin{cases}\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\ \ &\text{ if }\mu(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))\geq q\\
(2^{\omega})^{X}\ \ &\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(\neg A\wedge B)\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\wedge B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

\displaystyle=\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu(\Pi_{f}\big(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}\cap\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

\displaystyle=\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cap\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

There are two cases,

•

Let
\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\geq q
. Then,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(\neg A\wedge B)\rrbracket_{X}=\

\displaystyle\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cap\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{C}\penalty\ \ref{C1}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ f\not\in\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X}\vee\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ f\not\in\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X}\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ f\in\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}.

•

Let
\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q
.
Since
\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cap\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)

\leq

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)
,

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cap\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q
.
Consequently,
\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\cap\Pi_{f}(\llbracket B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

= (
2^{\omega}
)
X
.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(\neg A\wedge B)\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\ \ &\text{ if }\mu(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\neg B\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))\geq q\\
(2^{\omega})^{X}\ \ &\text{ otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\llbracket A\vee\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}B\rrbracket_{X}.

∎

See
11

Proof.

Let us first deal with:

\llbracket\neg\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}=\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}.

There are two cases:

•

If
q
= 0,

\displaystyle\llbracket\neg\mathbf{D}^{0}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle\ =(2^{\omega})^{X}\ –\ \llbracket\mathbf{D}^{0}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{L}\penalty\ \ref{Lq0}}}{{=}}(2^{\omega})^{X}\ –\ \emptyset^{X}

\displaystyle\ =(2^{\omega})^{X}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{L}\penalty\ \ref{Lq0}}}{{=}}\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{0}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

•

Otherwise,
q>
0,

\displaystyle\llbracket\neg\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=(2^{\omega})^{X}–\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=(2^{\omega})^{X}–\{f:X\rightarrow 2^{\omega}\ |\ \mu(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))<q\}

\displaystyle=\{f:X\rightarrow 2^{\omega}\ |\ \mu(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))\geq q\}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}.

Let us now consider:

\llbracket\neg\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}=\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}.

Again, there are two possible cases:

•

Let
q
= 0. Then,

\displaystyle\llbracket\neg\mathbf{C}^{0}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}=\

\displaystyle(2^{\omega})^{X}\ –\ \llbracket\mathbf{C}^{0}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{L}\penalty\ \ref{Lq0}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle(2^{\omega})^{X}\ –\ (2^{\omega})^{X}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\emptyset^{X}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{L}\penalty\ \ref{Lq0}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{0}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}.

•

Let
q>
0. Then,

\displaystyle\llbracket\neg\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=(2^{\omega})^{X}–\ \llbracket\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle=(2^{\omega})^{X}–\ \{f:X\rightarrow 2^{\omega}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\geq q\}

\displaystyle=\{f:X\rightarrow 2^{\omega}\ |\ \mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<q\}

\displaystyle=\llbracket\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}.

∎

See
12

In order to prove the given Proposition
14
,
some auxiliary definitions and lemmas have to be introduced.

Definition C.1

(Negation Simple Normal Form)
.

A formula of
CPL
is a
negation simple normal form
(NSNF)
if and only if negations only appear in front of atoms.

Lemma 41
.

Every formula of
CPL
can be converted into an equivalent formula in NSNF.

Proof.

For every formula of
CPL
, call it
A
, there is a NSNF,
call it
B
, such that, for every
X
with
\mathrm{FN}(A)\cup\mathrm{FN}(B)\subseteq X
,
A\equiv_{X}B
.
The proof is by induction on the weight of
A
.

•

Base case.

A=\mathbf{i}_{n}
, for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
B
=
A
, as
A
is already in NSNF.

•

Inductive case.

Assume that the procedure holds for formulas of weight up to
n
,
and show it to exist for formulas of weight
n
+1.
If
A\neq\neg C
, then the proof is trivial:
for IH there is a procedure for
C
, so the same holds for
A
. Let
A=\neg C
. There is a limited number of cases:

C=\neg C^{\prime}
.
For IH, there is a NSNF formula
D
such that
C^{\prime}\equiv_{X}D
. Let
B=D
. Since,
A=\neg C=\neg\neg C^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{\equiv}}_{X}\neg\neg D\equiv_{X}D
, and
D
is in NSNF for IH. So
A

\equiv_{X}
(
D
=)
B
and
B
is in NSNF.

C=C^{\prime}\vee C^{\prime\prime}.
So,
A=\neg(C^{\prime}\vee C^{\prime\prime})\equiv_{X}\neg C^{\prime}\wedge\neg C^{\prime\prime}
. For IH, there are two NSNF formulas
D^{\prime}
and
D^{\prime\prime}
such that
\neg C^{\prime}\equiv_{X}D^{\prime}
and
\neg C^{\prime\prime}\equiv_{X}D^{\prime\prime}
. Let
B=D^{\prime}\wedge D^{\prime\prime}
. As
A=\neg(C^{\prime}\vee C^{\prime\prime})\equiv_{X}\neg C^{\prime}\wedge\neg C^{\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{\equiv}}_{X}D^{\prime}\wedge D^{\prime\prime}
=
B
,
A\equiv_{X}B
and, since
D^{\prime}
and
D^{\prime\prime}
are in NSNF (IH)
B
is in NSNF as well.

C=C^{\prime}\wedge C^{\prime\prime}
. Equivalent to the previous case.

C=\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}C^{\prime}
. So,
A=\neg(\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}C^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lem}\penalty\ \ref{lemma:pseudoduality}}}{{\equiv}}\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}C^{\prime}
.
For IH, there is a NSNF
D
, such that
C^{\prime}\equiv_{X}D
. Let
B=\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}D
. As
A=\neg(\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}C^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lem}\penalty\ \ref{lemma:pseudoduality}}}{{\equiv}}_{X}\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(C^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{\equiv}}_{X}\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}D=B
,
A\equiv_{X}B
and, since
D
is in NSNF, also
B
=
\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}D
is in NSNF.

C=\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}C^{\prime}.
Equivalent to the previous case.

∎

Lemma 42
.

Every formula of
CPL
in NSNF can be converted into an equivalent formula in PNF (of the same or smaller weight).

Proof.

For every formula of
CPL
in NSNF, call it
A
, there is a PNF
B
such that, for every
X
with
\mathrm{FN}(A)\cup\mathrm{FN}(B)\subseteq X
,
A\equiv_{X}B
.
Assume
A
is in NSNF.
First, all bound variable are renamed with fresh ones.
Then, the lemma is proved by induction on the weight of
A
.

•

Base case.

A=\mathbf{i}_{n}
.
A
is already in PNF.

•

Inductive case.
Assume that the procedure holds for formulas of weight up to
n
, and show it to exist for formulas of weight
n
+1.

A=\neg C.
Since
A
is in NSNF,
C
must be atomic, so
A
=
\neg\mathbf{i}_{n}
(for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
) is already in PNF.

A=C\vee D.
For IH, there are two PNF formulas
C^{\prime}
and
D^{\prime}
such that
C\equiv_{X}C^{\prime}
and
D\equiv_{X}D^{\prime}
. There are some possible cases:

–

Both
C^{\prime}
and
D^{\prime}
do not contain
\triangle
. Let
B
=
C^{\prime}\vee D^{\prime}
.
B
is in PNF and
A\equiv_{X}B
(as
A
=
C\vee D

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{\equiv}}_{X}

C^{\prime}\vee D^{\prime}
=
B
).

–

C^{\prime}
=
\triangle C^{\prime\prime}
. If
\triangle
is of the form
\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}
, for Lemma
10
,

(\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}C^{\prime\prime})\vee D^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{L}\penalty\ \ref{lemma:commutations}}}{{\equiv}}\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}(C^{\prime\prime}\vee D^{\prime})

of the same weight. For IH,
C^{\prime\prime}\vee D^{\prime}
can be converted into a PNF formula,
B^{\prime}
. Let
B
=
\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}B^{\prime}
. Since
B^{\prime}
is in PNF, clearly also
B
must be. Indeed,

A=C\vee D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{\equiv}}_{X}(\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}C^{\prime\prime})\vee D^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{L}\penalty\ \ref{lemma:commutations}}}{{\equiv}}_{X}\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}(C^{\prime\prime}\vee D^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle IH}}{{\equiv}}_{X}\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}B^{\prime}=B.

If
\Delta
is of the form
\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a},
the proof is the same by applying both Lemma
10
(with,
\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}C\vee D\equiv_{X}\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}(\neg C\vee D)
and Lemma
41
).

A=C\wedge D
. The proof is similar to the previous one.

A=\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}D.
For IH, there is a
D^{\prime}
such that
D^{\prime}
is in PNF and
D\equiv_{X}D^{\prime}
. Let
B=\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}D^{\prime}
. Since
D^{\prime}
is in PNF, clearly
B
is in PNF as well. Furthermore, since
A=\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}
:

A=\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}D\equiv_{X}\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}D^{\prime}=B.

A=\mathbf{D}^{q}_{a}C.
The proof is equivalent to the previous one.

∎

Thus, every formula can be converted into an equivalent formula in PNF.

Proof of Proposition
14
.

By combining Lemma
41
and Lemma
42
.
∎

C.2
Positive Prenex Normal Form

See
13

To establish Lemma
13
we need a few preliminary lemmas. First of all,
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, let
[0,1]_{k}
indidate the set of rationals of the form

q=\sum_{i=0}^{k}b_{i}\cdot 2^{-i}
, where
b_{i}\in\{0,1\}
.

Lemma 43
.

For all
p\leq 2^{k}
,
\frac{p}{2^{k}}\in[0,1]_{k}
.

Proof.

Let
b_{0}\dots b_{k}=(p)_{2}
the base 2 writing of
p
(with possibly all 0s at the end so that the length is precisely
k
), then
p=\sum_{i=0}^{k}b_{i}\cdot 2^{i}
, so:

\displaystyle\frac{p}{2^{k}}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\sum_{i=0}^{k}b_{i}\cdot 2^{i}}{2^{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k}b_{i}\cdot 2^{-k+i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k}b_{k-i}\cdot 2^{-i}.

∎

Lemma 44
.

For all Boolean formula
b
with
\mathrm{FN}(b)\subseteq\{a\}
,
\mu(\llbracket b\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\in[0,1]_{k}
, where
k
is the maximum natural number such that
x_{k}^{a}
occurs in
b
.

Proof.

By Lemma
1
and Lemma
43
:

\mu(\llbracket b\rrbracket_{\{a\}})=\sharp b=\dfrac{\sharp\{m:\{x_{0}^{a},\dots,x_{k}^{a}\}\to\{0,1\}\mid m\vDash b\}}{2^{k}}\in[0,1]_{k}.

∎

Lemma 45
.

For all
S\in B\big((2^{\omega})^{X\cup\{a\}}\big)
and
f:X\to 2^{\omega}
,

\overline{\Pi_{f}(S)}=\Pi_{f}(\overline{S})
.

Proof.

Indeed:

\displaystyle\overline{\Pi_{f}(S)}

\displaystyle=\overline{\{g:\{a\}\to 2^{\omega}\mid f+g\in S\}}

\displaystyle=\{g:\{a\}\to 2^{\omega}\mid f+g\notin S\}

\displaystyle=\{g:\{a\}\to 2^{\omega}\mid f+g\in\overline{S}\}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{f}(\overline{S}).

∎

Lemma 46
.

For all
S\in B((2^{\omega})^{X})
and
r\in[0,1]
,

\mu(S)\leq r
iff
\mu(\overline{S})\geq 1-r
.

Proof.

The claim follows from:

1=\mu((2^{\omega})^{X})=\mu(S\cup\overline{S})=\mu(S)+\mu(\overline{S}).

∎

We have now all ingredients to prove Lemma
13
.

Proof of Lemma
13
.

Let
b_{A}
be a Boolean formula satisfying
\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}=\llbracket b_{A}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}
(which exists by Lemma
33
).
Let
b_{A}
be
a
-decomposable as
\bigvee_{i}^{n}d_{i}\land e_{i}
and let
k
be maximum such that
x_{k}^{a}
occurs in
b_{A}
.
By Lemma
44
for all
i=0,\dots,n
,
\mu(\llbracket d_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\in[0,1]_{k}
.
This implies in particular that for all
f:X\to 2^{\omega}
,
\mu(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))\in[0,1]_{k}
, since
\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}))
coincides with the unique
\llbracket d_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}}
such that
f\in\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{X}
(by Lemma
8
).
Now, if
r\notin[0,1]_{k}
, let
\epsilon=0
; and if
r\in[0,1]_{k}
, then
if
r=1
, let
\epsilon=-2^{-(k+1)}
, and if
r\neq 1
, let
\epsilon=2^{-(k+1)}
.
In all cases
r+\epsilon\notin[0,1]_{k}
, so we deduce

\displaystyle\llbracket\lnot\mathbf{C}^{r}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}

\displaystyle\ =\{f:X\to 2^{\omega}\mid\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)<r\}

\displaystyle\ =\{f:X\to 2^{\omega}\mid\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\leq r+\epsilon\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{L\penalty\ \ref{lemma:leq}}}}{{=}}\{f:X\to 2^{\omega}\mid\mu\big(\overline{\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})}\big)\geq 1-(r+\epsilon)\}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{L\penalty\ \ref{lemma:pif}}}}{{=}}\{f:X\to 2^{\omega}\mid\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\overline{\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})}\big)\geq 1-(r+\epsilon)\}

\displaystyle\ =\{f:X\to 2^{\omega}\mid\mu\big(\Pi_{f}({\llbracket\lnot A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})}\big)\geq 1-(r+\epsilon)\}

\displaystyle\ =\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{1-(r+\epsilon)}_{a}\lnot A\rrbracket_{X}.

∎

Appendix D
Proofs for Section
6

D.1
The Type System
\lambda\textsf{{CPL}}

D.1.1
Permutative Normal Forms

To characterize the
{PNF}
we introduce the following class of terms, called
(S,a)
-trees.

Definition D.1
.

Let
S
be a set of name-closed
\lambda
-terms and
a
be a name.
For all
i\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{-1\}
, the set of
(S,a)
-trees of level
i
is defined as follows:

•

any
t\in S
is a
(S,a)
-tree of level
-1
;

•

if
t,u
are
(S,a)
-trees of level
j
and
k
, respectively, and
j,k<i
, then
t\oplus_{a}^{i}u
is a
(S,a)
-tree of level
i
.

The
support
of a
(S,a)
-tree
t
, indicated as
\mathrm{Supp}(t)
, is the finite set of terms in
S
which are leaves of
t
.

Definition D.2
.

The sets
\mathcal{T}
and
\mathcal{V}
of name-closed terms are defined inductively as follows:

•

all variable
x\in\mathcal{V}
;

•

if
t\in\mathcal{V}
,
\lambda x.t\in\mathcal{V}
;

•

if
t\in\mathcal{V}
and
u\in\mathcal{T}
, then
tu\in\mathcal{V}
;

•

if
t\in\mathcal{V}
, then
t\in\mathcal{T}
;

•

if
t
is a
(\mathcal{T},a)
-tree, then
\nu a.t\in\mathcal{T}
.

Lemma 47
.

For all name-closed term
t\in\mathcal{T}
,
t\in\mathcal{V}
iff it does not start with
\nu
.

Proof.

First observe that if
t=\nu a.b
, then
t\notin\mathcal{V}
. For the converse direction, we argue by induction on
t\neq\nu a.b
:

•

if
t=x
then
t\in\mathcal{V}
;

•

if
t=\lambda x.u
then by IH
u\in\mathcal{V}
, so
t\in\mathcal{V}
;

•

if
t=uv
, then the only possibility is that
t\in\mathcal{V}
;

•

if
t=u\oplus_{a}^{i}v
, then
t
is not name-closed, again the hypothesis.

∎

For all term
t
and
a\in\mathrm{FN}(t)
, we let
I_{a}(t)
be the maximum index
i
such that
\oplus_{a}^{i}
occurs in
t
, and
I_{a}(t)=-1
if
\oplus_{a}^{i}
does not occur in
t
for all index
i
.

We now show that Definition
D.2
precisely captures
{PNF}
.

Lemma 48
.

A name-closed term
t
is in
{PNF}
iff
t\in\mathcal{T}
.

Proof.

(
\Rightarrow
)

We argue by induction on
t
.
If
t
has no bound name, then
t
is obviously in
\mathcal{T}
. Otherwise:

•

if
t=\lambda x.u
, then
u
is also name-closed. Hence, by IH
u\in\mathcal{T}
; observe that
u
cannot start with
\nu
(as
t
would not be normal) so by Lemma
47
,
u\in\mathcal{V}
, which implies
t\in\mathcal{T}
;

•

if
t=uv
, then
u
and
v
are both name-closed, and so by induction
u,v\in\mathcal{T}
; if
u
started with
\nu
, then
t
would not be normal, hence, by Lemma
47
,
u\in\mathcal{V}
, and we conclude then that
t\in\mathcal{T}
.

•

if
t=u\oplus_{a}^{i}v
, then it cannot be name-closed;

•

if
t=\nu a.u
, then we show, by a sub-induction on
u
, that
u
is a
(\mathcal{T},a)
-tree of level
I_{a}(u)
: first note that
I_{a}(u)
cannot be
-1
, since otherwise
\nu a.u\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}u
, so
u
would not be normal. We now consider all possible cases for
u
:

–

u
cannot be a variable, or
I_{a}(u)
would be
-1
;

–

If
u=\lambda x.v
, then
I_{a}(v)=I_{a}(u)
and so by sub-IH
v
is a
(\mathcal{T},a)
-tree of level
I_{a}(u)
, which implies that
u=\lambda x.v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{I_{a}(u)}v_{2}
, which is not normal. Absurd.

–

if
u=vw
, then let
J=I_{a}(u)=\max\{I_{a}(v),I_{a}(w)\}
cannot be
-1
, since otherwise
I_{a}(u)
would be
-1
. Hence
J\geq 0
is either
I_{a}(v)
or
I_{a}(w)
. We consider the two cases separatedly:

*

if
J=I_{a}(v)
, then by sub-IH,
v=v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}v_{2}
, and thus

u=(v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}v_{2})w
is not normal;

*

if
J=I_{a}(w)
, then by sub-IH,
w=w_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}w_{2}
, and thus

u=v(w_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}w_{2})
is not normal.

In any case we obtain an absurd conclusion.

–

if
u=u_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}u_{2}
, then if
u_{1},u_{2}
are both in
\mathcal{V}
, we are done, since
u
is a
(\mathcal{T},a)
-tree of level
i=I_{a}(u)
. Otherwise, if
i<I_{a}(u)
, then
J=I_{a}(u)=\max\{I_{a}(v),I_{a}(w)\}
, so we consider two cases:

*

if
J=I_{a}(v)>i
, then by sub-IH,
v=v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}v_{2}
, and thus

u=(v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}v_{2})\oplus_{a}^{i}u_{2}
is not normal;

*

if
J=I_{a}(w)>i
, then by sub-IH,
w=w_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}w_{2}
, and thus

u=v\oplus_{a}^{i}(w_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}w_{2})
is not normal.

We conclude then that
i=I_{a}(u)
; we must then show that
I_{a}(v),I_{a}(w)<i
. Suppose first
I_{a}(v)\geq i
, then
(v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{J}v_{2})\oplus_{a}^{i}u_{2}
is not normal. In a similar way we can show that

I_{a}(w)<i
.

–

if
u=\nu b.v
, then
I_{a}(u)=I_{a}(v)
, so by sub-IH
v=v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{I_{a}(u)}v_{2}
, and we conclude that
u=\nu b.v_{1}\oplus_{a}^{i}v_{2}
is not normal.

(
\Leftarrow
)

It suffices to check by induction on
t\in\mathcal{T}
that it is in
{PNF}
.

∎

Corollary 6
.

A name-closed term of the form
\nu a.t
is in
{PNF}
iff
t
is a
(\mathcal{T},a)
-tree of level
I_{a}(t)\geq 0
.

D.1.2
The Subject Reduction Property

We now establish the following standard property for
\lambda\textsf{{CPL}}
:

See
17

To establish the subject reduction property, we first need to establish a few auxiliary lemmas.

Lemma 49
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma
and
X\subseteq Y
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{Y,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma
.

Proof.

Straightforward induction on a type derivation of
t
.
∎

Lemma 50
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma
holds and
c\vDash^{X}b
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:c\rightarrowtail\sigma
is derivable.

Proof.

By induction on a type derivation of
t
:

•

if the last rule is

\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X

b\vDash^{X}\bot

\mathsf{R}_{\bot}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,q}t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, since
c\vDash^{X}b
and
b\vDash^{X}\bot
imply
c\vDash^{X}\bot
, we conclude by an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\mathsf{R}_{\mathrm{id}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,q\cdot s}x:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

where
r=q\cdot s
, the claim is immediately deduced by an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}t:b_{1}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}t:b_{2}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b_{1}\vee b_{2}

\mathsf{R}_{\cup}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}x:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, from
c\vDash^{X}b
we deduce
c\vDash^{X}b_{1}\vee b_{2}
so we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma,y:\mathfrak{s},\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}\lambda y.t:b\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\rho

Then, by IH, we deduce
\Gamma,y:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r}t[u/x]:c\rightarrowtail\rho
and we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X,q}t_{1}:b_{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{r}\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t_{2}:b_{2}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b_{1}\land b_{2}

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,q}t_{1}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\tau

then from
c\vDash^{X}b
and
b\vDash^{X}b_{1}\land b_{2}
we deduce

c\vDash^{X}b_{1}\land b_{2}
, so
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, from
c\vDash^{X}b
, we deduce
c\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}
, so
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{2}:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

we can argue similarly to the previous case.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq s

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu a.t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, from
c\vDash^{X}b
and
b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}
we deduce
c\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}
, so
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

∎

Lemma 51
.

If
\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\tau
and

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}u:c\rightarrowtail\sigma
, then

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t[u/x]:b\land c\rightarrowtail\tau
.

Proof.

We argue by induction on the typing derivation of
t
:

•

if the last rule is

\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X

b\vDash^{X}\bot

\mathsf{R}_{\bot}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,q\cdot s}t:b\rightarrowtail\tau

where
r=q\cdot s
, then, since
\mathrm{FN}({t[u/x]})\subseteq X
and
b\land c\vDash^{X}b\vDash^{X}\bot
the claim is deduced using an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\mathrm{FN}(b)\subseteq X

\mathsf{R}_{\mathrm{id}}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,s}x:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then,
t[u/x]=u
and
r=x
, so the claim is deduced from the hypothesis and Lemma
50
.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,s}t:b_{1}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,s}t:b_{2}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b_{1}\vee b_{2}

\mathsf{R}_{\cup}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,s}x:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, by IH, we deduce
\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,s}t[u/x]:b_{1}\land c\rightarrowtail\sigma

and
\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,s}t[u/x]:b_{2}\land c\rightarrowtail\sigma
and since

b\land c\vDash^{X}(b_{1}\land c)\vee(b_{2}\land c)
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma,y:\mathfrak{s},x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,r}\lambda y.t:b\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\rho

Then, by IH, we deduce
\Gamma,y:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r}t[u/x]:b\land c\rightarrowtail\rho
and since
(\lambda y.t)[u/x]=\lambda y.t[u/x]
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,q}t_{1}:b_{1}\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{r}\sigma\Rightarrow\tau

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,r}t_{2}:b_{2}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b_{1}\land b_{2}

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,q}t_{1}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\tau

Then, by IH, we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X,q}t_{1}[u/x]:b_{1}\land c\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{r}\sigma\Rightarrow\tau
and

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t_{2}[u/x]:b_{2}\land c\rightarrowtail\sigma
, and since
(t_{1}t_{2})[u/x]=(t_{1}[u/x])(t_{2}[u/x])
and

b\land c\vDash^{X}(b_{1}\land c)\land(b_{2}\land c)
,
we conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, by IH, we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}[u/x]:b^{\prime}\land c
.
From
t[u/x]=(t_{1}[u/x])\oplus_{a}^{i}(t_{2}[u/x])
and the fact that

b\land c\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}x_{i}^{a}\land(b^{\prime}\land c)
, we deduce the claim by an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{2}:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

we can argue similarly to the previous case.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq s

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma\vdash^{X,q\cdot r}\nu a.t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, by IH,

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t[u/x]:\left(\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\right)\land c\rightarrowtail\sigma
.
Now, from
\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land(d_{i}\land c)\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\left(\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\right)\land c
, using Lemma
57
, we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t[u/x]:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land(d_{i}\land c)\rightarrowtail\sigma
.
Hence, since

b\land c\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}(d_{i}\land c)
, and since

(\nu a.t)[u/x]=\nu a.t[u/x]
, we can deduce the clam by applying an instance of the same rule.

∎

Lemma 52
.

Let
b,b_{1},\dots,b_{n}
and
c,c_{1},\dots,c_{n}
be such that
\mathrm{FN}({b}),\mathrm{FN}({b_{i}})\subseteq{X}
and
\mathrm{FN}(c),\mathrm{FN}({c_{i}})\subseteq\{a\}
, where
a\notin X
.
If
c
is satisfiable, then if
b\land c\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}^{n}b_{i}\land c_{i}
holds,
also
b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}^{n}b_{i}
holds.

Proof.

Let
v\in 2^{X}
be a model of
b
. Since
c
is satisfiable,
v
can be extended to a model
v^{\prime}\in 2^{X\cup\{a\}}
of
b\land c
. By hypothesis, then
v^{\prime}
satisfies
\bigvee_{i}^{n}b_{i}\land c_{i}
, so for some
i_{0}\leq n
, it satisfies
b_{i_{0}}\land c_{i_{0}}
. We deduce then that
v
satisfies
b_{i_{0}}
, and thus
v
satisfies
\bigvee_{i}^{n}b_{i}
.
∎

Lemma 53
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho
and
\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X
, then for all weak
a
-decomposition
b\equiv_{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}
and
s\in[0,1]
,

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}t:\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\rightarrowtail\rho
.

Proof.

By induction on a type derivation of
t
:

•

if the last rule is

\mathrm{FN}{(t)}\subseteq X\cup\{a\}

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bot

R_{\bot}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

From
\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bot
we deduce

c_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bot
for all
i
. Since
c_{i}
and
d_{i}
have no propositional variable in common, we also deduce that either
c_{i}\vDash^{\{a\}}\bot
or
d_{i}\vDash^{X}\bot
.
By hypothesis, we deduce then that
d_{i}\vDash^{X}\bot
holds for all
i
and thus that

\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\vdash^{X}\bot
. Since moreover
\mathrm{FN}{(t)}\subseteq X
, the claim can be deduced by an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\mathrm{FN}(b)\subseteq X\cup\{a\}

\mathsf{R}_{\mathrm{id}}

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{r^{\prime}}\sigma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}x:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

where
r=r^{\prime}\cdot s^{\prime}
, then the claim can be deduced by an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:b_{1}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:b_{2}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}b_{1}\vee b_{2}

\mathsf{R}_{\cup}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}x:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Let
\bigvee_{j}c^{\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime}_{j}
and
\bigvee_{k}c^{\prime\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime\prime}_{k}
be weak
a
-decompositions of
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
.
By IH we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}t:\bigvee_{j}d^{\prime}_{j}\rightarrowtail\sigma
and

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}t:\bigvee_{k}d^{\prime\prime}_{k}\rightarrowtail\sigma
.

From
\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}b_{1}\vee b_{2}
we deduce
that for all
i
,

c_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\left(\bigvee_{j}c^{\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime}_{j}\right)\lor\left(\bigvee_{k}c^{\prime\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime\prime}_{k}\right)
, so by Lemma
52
we deduce that

d_{i}\vDash^{X}\left(\bigvee_{j}d^{\prime}_{j}\right)\lor\left(\bigvee_{k}d^{\prime\prime}_{k}\right)
, and finally that

\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\vDash^{X}\left(\bigvee_{j}d^{\prime}_{j}\right)\vee\left(\bigvee_{k}d^{\prime\prime}_{k}\right)

Hence the claim can be deduced by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma,y:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}\lambda y.t:b\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\rho

Then, the claim follows from the IH by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t_{1}:b_{1}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s^{\prime}}\sigma)\Rightarrow\tau

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},s^{\prime}}t_{2}:b_{2}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}b_{1}\land b_{2}

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t_{1}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\tau

Then,
let
\bigvee_{j}c^{\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime}_{j}
and
\bigvee_{k}c^{\prime\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime\prime}_{k}
be weak
a
-decompositions of
b_{1}
and
b_{2}
.
By IH we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}t:\bigvee_{j}d^{\prime}_{j}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s^{\prime}}\sigma)\Rightarrow\tau
and

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s^{\prime}}t:\bigvee_{k}d^{\prime\prime}_{k}\rightarrowtail\sigma
.

From
\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\left(\bigvee_{j}c^{\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime}_{j}\right)\land\left(\bigvee_{k}c^{\prime\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime\prime}_{k}\right)
we deduce that for all
i
,

\displaystyle c_{i}\land d_{i}

\displaystyle\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{j}c^{\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime}_{j}

\displaystyle c_{i}\land d_{i}

\displaystyle\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{k}c^{\prime\prime}_{k}\land d^{\prime\prime}_{k}

Using Lemma
52
we deduce then that

\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{j}d^{\prime}_{j}

and
\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{k}d^{\prime\prime}_{k}
hold, that is, that
\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\vDash^{X}\left(\bigvee_{j}d^{\prime}_{j}\right)\land\left(\bigvee_{k}d^{\prime\prime}_{k}\right)
holds.
We can thus conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t_{1}:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}x_{i}^{b}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{b}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, let
\bigvee_{j}c^{\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime}_{j}
be an
a
-decomposition of
b^{\prime}
and observe that

(x_{0}^{a}\land x_{i}^{b})\lor(\lnot x_{0}^{a}\land x_{i}^{b})
is an
a
-decomposition of
x_{i}^{b}
.
By IH we deduce that

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}t_{1}:\bigvee_{j}d^{\prime}_{j}\rightarrowtail\sigma
.

By reasoning as in the previous case, from
b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}x_{i}^{b}\land b^{\prime}
we deduce, using Lemma
52
, that
\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{b}\land\bigvee_{j}d^{\prime}_{j}
. We can thus conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t_{2}:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash\lnot x_{i}^{b}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{b}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, we can argue similarly to the previous case.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\},q}t:\bigvee_{u}f_{u}\land e_{u}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\mu(\llbracket f_{u}\rrbracket_{\{b\}})\geq s^{\prime}

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{u}e_{u}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},q\cdot s^{\prime}}\nu b.t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

Then, let
\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}
, be a weak
a
- decomposition of
b
. For all
u
, let
\bigvee_{j}c_{u,j}\land e_{u,j}
be an
a
-decomposition of
e_{u}
.
Then observe that we have

\displaystyle\bigvee_{u}f_{u}\land e_{u}

\displaystyle\equiv^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\}}\bigvee_{u}f_{u}\land\left(\bigvee_{j}c_{u,j}\land e_{u,j}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\}}\bigvee_{u}\bigvee_{j}f_{u}\land(c_{u,j}\land e_{u,j})

\displaystyle\equiv^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\}}\bigvee_{u,j}c_{u,j}\land(f_{u}\land e_{u,j})

So, by IH, we deduce that
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{b\},q\cdot s}t:\bigvee_{u,j}f_{u}\land e_{u,j}\rightarrowtail\sigma
.
Now, from
b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{u}e_{u}
we deduce

c_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{u}\bigvee_{j}c_{u,j}\land e_{u,j}
, and using Lemma
52
we conclude

\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{u}\bigvee_{j}e_{u,j}
. We can thus conclude by applying an instance of the same rule.

∎

We now have all ingredients to establish the subject reduction property of
\lambda\textsf{{CPL}}
.

Proof of Proposition
17
.

For the case of
\beta
-reduction it suffices to check the claim when
t
is a redex
(\lambda x.t_{1})t_{2}
and
u
is
t_{1}[t_{2}/x]
. From

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma
we deduce then that

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}\tau\vdash^{X,r}t_{1}:b_{1}\rightarrowtail\sigma
and

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}t_{2}:b_{2}\rightarrowtail\tau
hold,
where
b\vDash^{X}b_{1}\land b_{2}
.
From Lemma
51
we deduce then

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}u:b_{1}\land b_{2}\rightarrowtail\sigma
and from Lemma
50
we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}u:b\rightarrowtail\sigma
.

For permutative reduction we consider all rules one by one:

(
t\oplus_{a}^{i}t\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}t
)

The last rule of
t
is either

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus^{a}_{i}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho

or

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus^{a}_{i}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, in either case, from
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho
,
b\vDash^{X}b^{\prime}
, using Lemma
50
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho
.

(
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v
)

There are three possible sub-cases:

1.

the type derivation is as follows:

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b^{\prime}\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}:b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then from
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho
and since we have
b\vdash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v:b\rightarrowtail\rho
.

2.

the type derivation is as follows:

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}u:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b^{\prime}\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v:b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, from
b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}
and
b^{\prime}\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}
we deduce
b\vDash^{X}\bot
, so we conclude
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}v):b\rightarrowtail\rho
using one of the initial rules.

3.

the type derivation is as follows:

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}v:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}v:b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then from
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}u:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho
and
b\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v:b\rightarrowtail\rho
.

(
t\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v)\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}t\oplus_{a}^{i}v
)

Similar to the case above.

(
\lambda x.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}(\lambda x.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\lambda x.u)
)

There are two possible sub-cases:

1.

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:b\rightarrowtail\rho

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}\lambda x.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u):b\rightarrowtail(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\rho)

Then, we deduce

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}\lambda x.t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\rho)

b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(\lambda x.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\lambda x.u):b\rightarrowtail(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\rho)

2.

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r}u:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:b\rightarrowtail\rho

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}\lambda x.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u):b\rightarrowtail(\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\rho)

Then, we can argue similarly to the previous case.

(
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}(tv)\oplus_{a}^{i}(uv)
)

There are two possible sub-cases:

1.

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma)\Rightarrow\rho

b^{\prime}\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma)\Rightarrow\rho

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}v:c\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b^{\prime}\land c

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v:b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma)\Rightarrow\rho

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}v:c\rightarrowtail\sigma

b^{\prime}\land c\vDash^{X}b^{\prime\prime}\land c

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}tv:b^{\prime}\land c\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime}\land c

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(tv)\oplus_{a}^{i}(uv):b\rightarrowtail\rho

2.

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}u:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma)\Rightarrow\rho

b^{\prime}\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma)\Rightarrow\rho

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}v:c\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b^{\prime}\land c

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)v:b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, we can argue similarly to the previous case.

(
t(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v)\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}(tu)\oplus_{a}^{i}(tv)
)

As before, there are two sub-cases:

1.

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma)\Rightarrow\rho

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}u:c^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

c\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land c^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}u\oplus_{a}^{i}v:c\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b^{\prime}\land c

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v):b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, we deduce that

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma)\Rightarrow\rho

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}u:c^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b^{\prime}\land c^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}tu:b\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}(tu)\oplus_{a}^{i}(tv):b\rightarrowtail\sigma

2.

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}\sigma)\Rightarrow\rho

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}v:c^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

c\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land c^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}u\oplus_{a}^{i}v:c\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X}b^{\prime}\land c

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v):b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, we can argue similarly to the previous case.

(
(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{b}^{j}v\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}v)\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{b}^{j}v)
)

We suppose here
a\neq b
or
i<j
. There are three sub-cases:

1.

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b^{\prime}\vDash^{X}x_{a}^{i}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{b}^{j}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{b}^{j}v:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{b}^{j}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{b}^{j}v:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{a}^{i}\land b

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{b}^{j}v):b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

2.

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}u:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b^{\prime}\vDash^{X}\lnot x_{a}^{i}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{b}^{j}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{b}^{j}v:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, we can argue similarly to the previous case.

3.

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}v:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{b}^{j}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\oplus_{b}^{j}v:b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then, we deduce (using the fact that
b\equiv_{X}(x_{i}^{a}\land b)\vee(\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b)
)

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}v:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{b}^{j}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t\oplus_{b}^{j}v:b\rightarrowtail\rho

x_{i}^{a}\land b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}v)\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{b}^{j}v):x_{i}^{a}\land b\rightarrowtail\rho

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}v:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}x_{b}^{j}\land b^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}u\oplus_{b}^{j}v:b\rightarrowtail\rho

\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b\vDash^{X}x_{i}^{a}\land b

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}v)\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{b}^{j}v):\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}(x_{i}^{a}\land b)\lor(\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b)

\mathsf{R}_{\cup}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}v)\oplus_{a}^{i}(u\oplus_{b}^{j}v):b\rightarrowtail\rho

(
t\oplus_{b}^{j}(u\oplus_{a}^{i}v)\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}u)\oplus_{a}^{i}(t\oplus_{b}^{j}v)
)

Similar to the case above.

(
\nu b.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u)\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}(\nu b.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\nu b.u)
)

We suppose
a\neq b
.
There are two sub-cases:

1.

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\},r}t:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\}}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\},r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\left(\mu(\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{\{b\}})\geq s\right)_{i\leq k}

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu b.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u):b\rightarrowtail\sigma

then from
\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{b\}}x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}
, we deduce
\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\}}\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land x_{i}^{a}\land d_{i}
,
and thus
e_{i}\land d_{i}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\}}\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land x_{i}^{a}\land d_{i}
, and since
a\neq b
,
d_{i}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\}}(\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i})\land x_{i}^{a}
.
Hence from
b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}
we deduce

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\land x_{i}^{a}
.
Moreover, from
\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{b\}}b^{\prime\prime}
, using Lemma
57
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\},r}t:\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma
.
We finally deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\},r}t:\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\left(\mu(\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{\{b\}})\geq s\right)_{i\leq k}

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r\cdot s}\nu b.t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}x_{i}^{a}\land b

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r\cdot s}(\nu b.t)\oplus_{a}^{i}(\nu b.u):b\rightarrowtail\sigma

2.

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\},r}u:b^{\prime\prime}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\}}\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b^{\prime\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}\cup\{b\},r}t\oplus_{a}^{i}u:\bigvee_{i}e_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\left(\mu(\llbracket e_{i}\rrbracket_{\{b\}})\geq s\right)_{i\leq k}

b\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu b.(t\oplus_{a}^{i}u):b\rightarrowtail\sigma

The we can argue similarly to the previous case.

(
\nu a.t\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}t
)

We have

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq s

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu a.t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

then by Lemma
53
we deduce
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}t:\bigvee_{i}d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma
, and from

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}
the claim can be deduced using Lemma
57
.

(
\lambda x.\nu a.t\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}\nu a.\lambda x.t
)

We have

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq s

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu a.t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\lambda x.\nu a.t:b\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\sigma

from which we deduce:

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\sigma

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}\lambda x.t:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\sigma

\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq s

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma,x:\mathfrak{s}\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu a.\lambda x.t:b\rightarrowtail\mathfrak{s}\Rightarrow\sigma

(
(\nu a.t)u\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}\nu a.(tu)
)

We have

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s^{\prime}}\tau)\Rightarrow\sigma

\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq s

b^{\prime}\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu a.t:b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s^{\prime}}\tau)\Rightarrow\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s^{\prime}}u:e\rightarrowtail\tau

b\vDash^{X}b^{\prime}\land e

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}(\nu a.t)u:b\rightarrowtail\sigma

From Lemma
49
we deduce then
\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},s^{\prime}}u:c\rightarrowtail\tau
; moreover,
we have

\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land(d_{i}\land e)\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\left(\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\right)\land e
and

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}(d_{i}\land e)
,
so we deduce

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}t:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s^{\prime}}\tau)\Rightarrow\sigma

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},s^{\prime}}u:e\rightarrowtail\tau

\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land(d_{i}\land e)\vDash^{X\cup\{a\}}\left(\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\right)\land e

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}tu:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land(d_{i}\land e)\rightarrowtail\sigma

\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq s

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}(d_{i}\land e)

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu a.(tu):b\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s^{\prime}}\tau)\Rightarrow\sigma

∎

D.1.3
The Normalization Theorem

We now establish our main result about
\lambda\textsf{{CPL}}
:

See
18

Before getting to the actual proof of Theorem
18
we need to establish some preliminary lemmas.
For all
r\in[0,1]
, let
\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
indicate the set of name-closed terms which reduce to a
{PNF}
in normal form with
\mathbf{P}\geq r

Lemma 54
.

If
tx\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
, then
t\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
.

Proof.

It is clear that for all variables
x,y
,
u
is a
{PNF}
normal with probability
\geq r
iff
u[x\mapsto y]
is a
{PNF}
normal with probability
\geq r
.
Suppose
tx
reduces to a
{PNF}

u
which is normal with probability
\geq r
. Then two cases arise:

•

u=u^{\prime}x
where
t
reduces to
u^{\prime}
. Then
u^{\prime}
is also a
{PNF}
in normal form with same probability as
u^{\prime}x
, and thus
t\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
;

•

tx
reduces to
(\lambda y.t^{\prime})x
, and
t^{\prime}[y\mapsto x]
reduces to
u
. Then

t
reduces to the
{PNF}

u[x\mapsto y]
.

∎

Definition D.3
.

A name-closed term
t
is said
neutral
when it is of the form
xt_{1}\dots t_{n}
, where
t_{1},\dots,t_{n}\in\mathcal{T}
.

Observe that a neutral term
t
is in head normal form, and thus
\sum_{v\in\mathcal{HN}}\mathcal{D}_{t}(v)=1
.

Lemma 55
.

If
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
then for all
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(t)\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
.

Proof.

We show by induction on
\rho
the following facts:

1.

for all

t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
and
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(S)\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
;

2.

for all neutral term
t
,
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.

•

if
\rho=o
, then by definition
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
iff for all
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(t)\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
. Moreover if
t
is neutral then for all
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(t)=t\in\mathcal{HN}
, so
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.

•

if
\rho=(\mathbf{C}^{s}\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\rho_{2}
and
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
, then since by the IH
x\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S)
,
tx\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S)
, so by the induction hypothesis for all
f\in S
,

\pi^{f}(tx)=\pi^{f}(t)x\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
, whence
\pi^{f}(t)\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
by Lemma
54
.

Moreover, for all
neutral term
xt_{1}\dots t_{n}
,
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
,
f\in S^{\prime}
, and
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S^{\prime})
,

xt_{1}\dots t_{n}\pi^{f}(u)
is also neutral; by the induction hypothesis we have then

\pi^{f}(xt_{1}\dots t_{n}u)=xt_{1}\dots t_{n}\pi^{f}(u)\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
, and we conclude that
xt_{1}\dots t_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.

∎

Lemma 56
.

\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(\emptyset)=\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,0}(S)=\{t\in\Lambda_{\nu}\mid\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X\}
.

Proof.

Let
\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}=\{t\in\Lambda_{\nu}\mid\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X\}
.
By induction on
\rho
: if
\rho=o
, then trivially for all
f\in\emptyset
and
t\in\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}
,
\pi^{f}(t)\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
, so
\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}\subseteq\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
.
Moreover, since for all
t\in\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}
and
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(t)\in\mathrm{Norm}^{0}
, also
\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}\subseteq\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,0}(S)
holds. The converse direction is immediate.

If
\rho=(\mathbf{C}^{s}\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\rho_{2}
, then for all
t\in\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}
, and
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(\emptyset)
,
tu\in\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}
, so by IH
tu\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
.
This shows in particular that for all
S^{\prime}\subseteq\emptyset
,
tu\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
, so we conclude that
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(\emptyset)
. The converse direction is again immediate.
Moreover, for all
t\in\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}
, and
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S)
,
tu\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,0}(S)
, since by IH
\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}\subseteq\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,0}(S)
, and certainly
tu\in\Lambda_{\nu}^{X}
.
This shows in particular that for all
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
,
tu\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,0}(S^{\prime})
, so we conclude that
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,0}(S)
. The converse direction is again immediate.

∎

Lemma 57
.

For all term
t
such that
\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X
,

t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
iff for all
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(t)\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
.

Proof.

If
\rho=o
, the claim follows from the definition.
If
\rho=(\mathbf{C}^{s}.\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\rho_{2}
, suppose
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
, let
f\in S
and
u\in\mathrm{Red}^{\emptyset,s}_{\rho_{1}}
. Then
u
is name-closed hence for all
g\in S
,
\pi^{g}(u)=u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
, which by the IH, implies
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S)
; we deduce then that
tu\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S)
, so by IH
\pi^{f}(tu)=\pi^{f}(t)u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{\emptyset,r}
. We can thus conclude that
\pi^{f}(t)\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
.

Conversely, suppose that for all
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(t)\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
, let
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
and
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S^{\prime})
; if
f\in S^{\prime}
, then by IH
\pi^{f}(u)\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{\emptyset,s}
, so
\pi^{f}(tu)=\pi^{f}(t)\pi^{f}(u)\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{\emptyset,r}
; we have thus proved that for all
f\in S^{\prime}
,
\pi^{f}(tu)\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{\emptyset,r}
, which by IH implies that
tu\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S^{\prime})
. We conclude then that
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.
∎

Lemma 58
.

If
t\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
and
t^{\prime}\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}t
, then
t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.

Proof.

Simple induction on
\rho
.
∎

We still need a few lemmas that will be essential for the inductive steps of the proof of Theorem
18
.

Lemma 59
.

For all terms
t,u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
with
\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X\cup\{a\}
,
\mathrm{FN}(u_{1}),\dots,\mathrm{FN}(u_{n})\subseteq X
, and measurable sets
S\in\mathcal{B}((2^{\omega})^{X\cup\{a\}})
and
S^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}((2^{\omega})^{X})
, if

1.

for all
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(tu_{1}\dots u_{n})\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
;

2.

for all
f\in S^{\prime}
,

\mu(\Pi^{f}(S))\geq s
;

then for all
f\in S^{\prime}
,
\pi^{f}((\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n})\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r\cdot s}
.

Proof.

We suppose
s>0
since otherwise the claim is trivial. Let us first consider the case where
n=0
.
Let
f\in S^{\prime}
. Since any term reduces to a (unique)
{PNF}
, we can suppose w.l.o.g. that the name-closed term
\pi^{f}(\nu a.t)=\nu a.t^{*}
is in
{PNF}
. By Corollary
6

t^{*}
is a
(\mathcal{T},a)
-tree of level
I_{a}(t^{*})
.
Observe that for all
g\in(2^{\omega})^{\{a\}}
,

\pi^{g}(t^{*})=\pi^{g}(\pi^{f}(t))=\pi^{g+f}(t)
.

If
N
is the cardinality of
\mathrm{Supp}(t^{*})
, by 2. and the fact that
s>0
we deduce that there exists a finite number
K
of elements
w_{1},\dots,w_{K}
of
\mathrm{Supp}(t^{*})
which are in
\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
, so that

\sum_{w\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}}\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{*})=w\}=\sum_{{i}=1}^{K}\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{*})=w_{i}\}\geq s

Now, by reducing each such terms
w_{i}
in
\nu a.t
to some
{PNF}

w^{\prime}_{i}
which is in normal form with probability
\geq r
(that is, such that
\sum_{u\in\mathcal{HN}}\mathcal{D}_{w^{\prime}_{i}}(u)\geq r
), we obtain a new
{PNF}

\nu a.t^{\sharp}
and we can compute

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in\mathcal{HN}}\mathcal{D}_{\nu a.t^{\sharp}}(u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in\mathcal{HN}}\left(\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(t^{\sharp})}\mathcal{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)\cdot\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{\sharp})=t^{\prime}\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(t^{\sharp})}\left(\sum_{u\in\mathcal{HN}}\mathcal{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)\cdot\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{\sharp})=t^{\prime}\}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Supp}(t^{\sharp})}\left(\sum_{u\in\mathcal{HN}}\mathcal{D}_{t^{\prime}}(u)\right)\cdot\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{\sharp})=t^{\prime}\}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{{i}=1}^{K}\left(\sum_{u\in\mathcal{HN}}\mathcal{D}_{w^{\prime}_{i}}(u)\right)\cdot\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{\sharp})=w^{\prime}_{i}\}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{{i}=1}^{K}r\cdot\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{\sharp})=w^{\prime}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=r\cdot\sum_{{i}=1}^{K}\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{\sharp})=w^{\prime}_{i}\}

\displaystyle=r\cdot\sum_{{i}=1}^{K}\mu\{g\mid\pi^{g}(t^{*})=w_{i}\}

\displaystyle\geq r\cdot s

For the case in which
n>0
, we argue as follows:
from the hypotheses we deduce by the first point that

\nu a.(tu_{1}u_{2}\dots u_{n})\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r\cdot s}
. We can then conclude by observing that

(\nu a.t)u_{1}u_{2}\dots u_{n}\rightarrow_{\mathsf{p}}\nu a.(tu_{1}u_{2}\dots u_{n})
.

∎

Lemma 60
.

For all terms
t,u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
with
\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X\cup\{a\}
,
\mathrm{FN}(u_{i})\subseteq X
,
and measurable sets
S\in B((2^{\omega})^{X\cup\{a\}})
and
S^{\prime}\subseteq B((2^{\omega})^{X})
, if

1.

tu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X\cup\{a\},r}(S)
;

2.

for all
f\in S^{\prime}
,
\mu(\Pi^{f}(S))\geq s
;

then
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r\cdot s}(S^{\prime})
.

Proof.

If
s=0
the claim follows from Lemma
56
, so suppose
s>0
.
We argue by induction on
\rho
:

•

if
\rho=o
, then the claim follows from Lemma
59
.

•

if
\rho=(\mathbf{C}^{s^{\prime}}\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\rho_{2}
, then let
S^{\prime\prime}\subseteq S^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s^{\prime}}(S^{\prime\prime})
.
Since
s>0
for all
f\in S^{\prime\prime}\subseteq S^{\prime}
there exists at least one
g\in(2^{\omega})^{\{a\}}
, such that
g+f\in\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}
.
Let
T=\{g+f\in S\mid f\in S^{\prime\prime}\}
.
T
is measurable, since it is the counter-image of a measurable set through a measurable function (the projection function from
(2^{\omega})^{X\cup\{a\}}
to
(2^{\omega})^{X}
).

We have then that
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s^{\prime}}(T)
: for all
g+f\in S^{\prime\prime}
,
\pi^{g+f}(u)=\pi^{f}(u)\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{\emptyset,s^{\prime}}
, so by Lemma
57
,
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s^{\prime}}(T)
.
Then using the hypothesis 1. we deduce that
tu_{1}\dots u_{n}u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,s^{\prime}}(T)
; moreover, for all
f\in S^{\prime\prime}
,
\mu(\Pi^{f}(T))=\mu(\Pi^{f}(S))\geq s
. So, by the induction hypothesis
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r\cdot s}(S^{\prime\prime})
.
We can thus conclude that
(\nu a.t)u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r\cdot s}(S^{\prime})
.

∎

Lemma 61
.

If
t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
, then
(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.

Proof.

By induction on
\rho
.
If
\rho=o
, then if
t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
, then for all
f\in S
,

\pi^{f}(t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n})=\pi^{f}(t)[\pi^{f}(u)/x]\pi^{f}(u_{1})\dots\pi^{f}(u_{n})\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
, that is
t^{\prime}
normalizes to a
r
-head normal form. It is clear then that
\pi^{f}(t^{\prime\prime})
, where

t^{\prime\prime}=(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n})
, normalizes to the same
r
-normal form, so
\pi^{f}(t^{\prime\prime})\in\mathrm{Norm}^{r}
, and we conclude that

t^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.

If
\rho=(\mathbf{C}^{s}.\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\rho_{2}
, then let
S^{\prime}\subseteq S
and
v\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S^{\prime})
; then
t[u/x]u_{1}\dots u_{n}v\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,s}(S^{\prime})
so by IH
(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}v\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,s}(S^{\prime})
. This proves in particular that
(\lambda x.t)uu_{1}\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,s}(S)
.
∎

Lemma 62
.

For all
t
such that
\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X
and measurable sets
S,S^{\prime}\in B((2^{\omega})^{X})
, if for all
S^{\prime\prime}\subseteq S^{\prime}
and

u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S^{\prime\prime})
,
t[u/x]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S\cap S^{\prime\prime})
, then
\lambda x.t\in\mathrm{Red}_{(\mathbf{C}^{s}.\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S\cap S^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Let
T\subseteq S\cap S^{\prime}
and
u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(T)
.
Then, by hypothesis,
t[u/x]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S\cap T)=\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S\cap S^{\prime})
and we conclude by Lemma
61
that
(\lambda x.t)u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S\cap S^{\prime})
.
This proves that
\lambda x.t\in\mathrm{Red}_{(\mathbf{C}^{s}.\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S\cap S^{\prime})
.

∎

We can now finally prove Theorem
18
, that will descend from the following:

Proposition 63
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho
, where
\Gamma=\{x_{1}:\mathbf{C}^{s_{1}}\rho_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\mathbf{C}^{s_{n}}\rho_{n}\}
, then for all
S\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}
and
u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s_{1}}(S),\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{n}}^{X,s_{n}}(S)
,
t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.

Proof.

The argument is by induction on a type derivation of
t
:

•

If the last rule is:

\mathrm{FV}(t)\subseteq\Gamma,\mathrm{FN}(t)\subseteq X

b\vDash^{X}\bot

\mathsf{R}_{\bot}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho

then from
S\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\emptyset
we deduce
S=\emptyset
, so can conclude by Lemma
56
.

•

if the last rule is

\mathrm{FN}(b)\subseteq X

\mathsf{R}_{\mathrm{id}}

\Gamma,x:(\mathbf{C}^{r}.\rho)\vdash^{X,r}x:b\rightarrowtail\rho

Then for all
S\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}
, and for all

u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,s_{1}}_{\rho_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,s_{n}}_{\rho_{n}}(S)
, and

u\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,r}_{\rho}(S)
,

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n},u/x]=u\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,s}(\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\cap S)=\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,s}(S)
.

•

if the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b_{1}\rightarrowtail\rho

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b_{2}\rightarrowtail\rho

b\vDash^{X}b_{1}\vee b_{2}

\mathsf{R}_{\cup}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\rho

then by IH and Lemma
55
for all
S\subseteq\llbracket b_{1}\rrbracket_{X}
, for all
u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s_{1}}(S),\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{n}}^{X,s_{n}}(S)
and for all
f\in S
,

\pi^{f}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
; similarly,
for all
S\subseteq\llbracket b_{2}\rrbracket_{X}
, for all
u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s_{1}}(S),\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{n}}^{X,s_{n}}(S)
and for all
f\in S
,

\pi^{f}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
.

Let now
S\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket b_{1}\rrbracket_{X}\cup\llbracket b_{2}\rrbracket_{X}
and
f\in S
.
If
f\in\llbracket b_{1}\rrbracket_{X}
, then first observe that from
u_{i}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{i}}^{X,s_{i}}(S)
it follows in particular that

u_{i}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{i}}^{X,s_{i}}(S\cap\llbracket b_{1}\rrbracket_{X})
: for all
g\in S\cap\llbracket b_{1}\rrbracket_{X}
, since
g\in S
,
\pi^{g}(u_{i})\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{i}}^{\emptyset,s_{i}}
, so by Lemma
57
we conclude that
u_{i}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{i}}^{X,s_{i}}(S\cap\llbracket b_{1}\rrbracket_{X})
.
Hence we deduce that

\pi^{f}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
.
If
f\in\llbracket b_{2}\rrbracket_{X}
by a similar argument we deduce
\pi^{f}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
.
Hence for all
f\in S
,
\pi^{f}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{\emptyset,r}
, and we conclude that

t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(S)
.

•

t=\lambda x.u
is obtained by

\Gamma,x:\mathbf{C}^{s}.\rho\vdash^{X,r}u:b\rightarrowtail\rho^{\prime}

\mathsf{R}_{\lambda}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}\lambda x.u:b\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}.\rho)\Rightarrow\rho^{\prime}

By IH, for all
S\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}
, for all

u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s_{1}}(S),\dots,u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{n}}^{X,s_{n}}(S)
,
and
v\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,s}(S)
,

u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n},v/x]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{X,r}(\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\cap S)
.
In particular, for all choice of
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
and variable
y
not occurring free in
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
, by letting
t^{\prime}=u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n},y/x]
, we have that for all

v\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,s}(S)
,

t^{\prime}[v/y]=u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n},v/x]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{X,r}(\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\cap S)
.
By Lemma
62
, then, we can conclude that
\lambda y.t^{\prime}=(\lambda y.t[y/x])[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]=(\lambda x.t)[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{(\mathbf{C}^{s}.\rho)\Rightarrow\rho^{\prime}}^{X,r}(\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\cap S)
.

•

t=uv
is obtained by

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}u:c\rightarrowtail(\mathbf{C}^{s}.\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\rho_{2}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,s}v:d\rightarrowtail\rho_{1}

b\vDash^{X}c\land d

\mathsf{R}_{@}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}uv:b\rightarrowtail\rho_{2}

By IH, for all

S\subseteq\llbracket c\rrbracket_{X}
,
for all
u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,s_{1}}_{\rho_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,s_{n}}_{\rho_{n}}(S)
and
w\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S)
,

u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]w\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S)
, and
for all

S\subseteq\llbracket d\rrbracket_{X}
,
for all
u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta,s_{1}}_{\rho_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta,s_{n}}_{\rho_{n}}(S)
,

v[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{1}}^{X,s}(S)
.
Let now
S\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\cap\llbracket c\rrbracket_{X}
and
u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta,s_{1}}_{\rho_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{\theta,s_{n}}_{\rho_{n}}(S)
. Then we deduce

u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]v[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]=uv[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{2}}^{X,r}(S)
.

•

if
t=t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}
, and the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t_{1}:b\rightarrowtail\rho

c\vDash(\lnot x_{i}^{a}\land b)

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{l}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t_{1}\oplus^{a}_{n}t_{2}:c\rightarrowtail\rho

By IH and Lemma
57
, for all

S\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}
,
f\in S
,

u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,s_{1}}_{\rho_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,s_{n}}_{\rho_{n}}(S)
,

\pi^{f}(t_{1}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{Red}^{\emptyset,r}_{\rho}
.
Let now
S\subseteq\llbracket c\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket x_{i}^{a}\rrbracket_{X}\land\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}
and
u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,s_{1}}_{\rho_{1}}(S),

\dots,

u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X,s_{n}}_{\rho_{n}}(S)
.
If
f\in S
, then
f(a)=0
so in particular
\pi^{f}(t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])=\pi^{f}(t_{1}[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}])\in\mathrm{Red}^{\emptyset,r}_{\rho}
.
Hence, using Lemma
57
we conclude that
t[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,

u_{n}/x_{n}]

\in

\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r}(\llbracket c\rrbracket_{X}\cap S)
.

•

if
t=t_{1}\oplus^{i}_{a}t_{2}
, and the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t_{2}:b\rightarrowtail\rho

c\vDash(x_{i}^{a}\land b)

\mathsf{R}_{\oplus}^{\mathrm{r}}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t_{1}\oplus^{a}_{n}t_{2}:c\rightarrowtail\rho

then we can argue similarly to the previous case.

•

If
t=\nu a.u
and the last rule is

\Gamma\vdash^{X\cup\{a\},r}u:\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rightarrowtail\rho

\vDash\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{X})\geq s

b\vDash\bigvee_{i}d_{i}

\mathsf{R}_{\nu}

\Gamma\vdash^{X,r\cdot s}\nu a.u:b\rightarrowtail\rho

where
a\notin FV(\Gamma)\cup FV(d_{i})
and
c=\bigvee_{i}c_{i}\land d_{i}
, then let
S\subseteq\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}
and
u_{1}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X\cup\{a\},s_{1}}_{\rho_{1}}(S),\dots u_{n}\in\mathrm{Red}^{X\cup\{a\},s_{n}}_{\rho_{n}}(S)
.
Let
T=\{g+f\mid f\in S\}
, which is measurable since counter-imaged of a measurable set through the projection function; then since
\mathrm{FN}(u_{i})\subseteq X
, we deduce using Lemma
55
that
u_{i}\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho_{i}}^{X,s_{i}}(T\cap\llbracket c\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})
.
By IH and the hypothesis we deduce then that

–

u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}^{X\cup\{a\},r}_{\rho}(T\cup\llbracket c\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})
;

–

for all
f\in S
,
\mu(\Pi^{f}(T)\cap\llbracket c\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\geq s
;

Hence, by Lemma
60
we conclude that

\nu a.u[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]=(\nu a.u)[u_{1}/x_{1},\dots,u_{n}/x_{n}]\in\mathrm{Red}_{\rho}^{X,r\cdot s}(S)
.

∎

D.2
The Curry-Howard Correspondence for
\lambda\textsf{{CPL}}

Proposition 64
.

If
\Gamma\vdash^{X,r}t:b\rightarrowtail\sigma
is derivable in
\lambda\textsf{{CPL}}
, then

\vdash^{X}_{\textsf{{CPL}}}b\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{r}_{a}\sigma^{*},\bot\leftarrowtail\Gamma^{*}
.

The embedding relies on some properties of
CPL
established in previous sections, as well as on the following few simple lemmas.
Preliminarily notice that, as anticipated,
we will define an embedding between
\lambda\textsf{{CPL}}
and

CPL
, by considering a multi-succedent calculus, which is
sound and complete for the semantics
CPL
, but is slightly different with respect to the one introduced in Section
4
.
We defined its rules by simply adding a set of labelled formulas,
call it
\Delta
, to the succedent of each premiss and conclusion.
The only rules which change a bit are
\mathsf{R1_{\vee}^{\rightarrowtail}}/\mathsf{R2_{\vee}^{\rightarrowtail}}
and
\mathsf{R1_{\wedge}^{\leftarrowtail}}/\mathsf{R2_{\wedge}^{\leftarrowtail}}
.
These are, respectively, substituted with the following multi-succedent rules:

\vdash^{X}b\rightarrowtail A,b\rightarrowtail B,\Delta

\mathsf{R}_{\vee}^{\rightarrowtail}

\vdash^{X}b\rightarrowtail A\vee B,\Delta

\vdash^{X}b\leftarrowtail A,b\leftarrowtail B,\Delta

\mathsf{R}_{\wedge}^{\leftarrowtail}

\vdash^{X}b\leftarrowtail A\wedge B,\Delta

Lemma 65
.

For all simple type
\sigma
,
q\in\mathbb{Q}_{[0,1]}
, name
a
,
and
b
with
\mathrm{FN}(b)\subseteq X
,

\vdash^{X}_{\textsf{{CPL}}}b\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}\sigma^{*},\Gamma
.

Proof.

Simple check by induction on
\sigma
.
∎

Lemma 66
.

The following rule is admissible in
\mathbf{GsCPL}
:

\vdash^{X\cup\{a\}}b^{\prime}\rightarrowtail A,\Delta

\left(\mu(\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq r\right)_{i\leq k}

b\vDash^{X}\bigvee_{i}^{k}d_{i}

\vdash^{X}b\rightarrowtail\mathbf{C}^{r}_{a}A,\Delta

where
\bigvee_{i}^{k}c_{i}\land d_{i}
is a weak
a
-decomposition of
b^{\prime}
.

Proof.

First observe that if
r=0
,
\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{r}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}=(2^{\omega})^{X}
, so the rule is trivially admissible. Suppose then
r>0
, let the premises of the rule be valid, and let all labelled formulas in
\Delta
not be valid.
We first show that
\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{r}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}
:
if
f\in\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}
, then for some
i
,
f\in\llbracket d_{i}\rrbracket_{X}
, hence by hypothesis

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket\bigvee_{i}^{k}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\big)\geq r
.
From
\llbracket\bigvee_{i}^{k}c_{i}\land d_{i}\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}\subseteq\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}
,
we deduce then also

\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\geq r
,
so we conclude
f\in\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{r}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}
.
From this fact, using Lemma
8
, we deduce that, if
\bigvee_{j}c^{\prime}_{j}\land d^{\prime}_{j}
is an
a
-decomposition of
\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}}
, then
\llbracket b\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\{\llbracket d^{\prime}_{j}\rrbracket_{X}\mid\mu(\llbracket c^{\prime}_{i}\rrbracket_{\{a\}})\geq r\}
.
So we can deduce the conclusion of the rule using an instance of the rule
\mathsf{R}_{\mathbf{C}}^{\rightarrowtail}
.
∎

Lemma 67
.

If
a\notin\mathrm{FN}(A)
, then
\mathbf{C}^{s}_{a}\mathbf{C}^{q}_{b}A\vDash\mathbf{C}^{q\cdot s}_{b}A
.

Proof.

If
a\notin\mathrm{FN}(A)
, we have that
\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}A\equiv^{X}A
.
In fact,

\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{q}_{a}A\rrbracket_{X}=\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\mid\mu\big(\Pi_{f}(\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X\cup\{a\}})\big)\geq q\}=\{f\in(2^{\omega})^{X}\mid f\in\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}\}=\llbracket A\rrbracket_{X}
.
Hence, we have

\mathbf{C}^{s}_{a}\mathbf{C}^{q}_{b}A\equiv_{X}\mathbf{C}^{q}_{b}A
. Moreover, since
q\cdot s\leq q
,

\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{q}_{b}A\rrbracket_{X}\subseteq\llbracket\mathbf{C}^{q\cdot s}_{b}A\rrbracket_{X}
.
∎

.

We define the embedding by induction on the typing rules of
\lambda\textsf{{CPL}}

Counting Rule
